def possible_pairs(limit=20):
    res=[]
    for k in range(2,limit+1):
        for l in range(2,limit+1):
            m=1+k+l*k
            r=k*l/m
            res.append((k,l,m,r))
    # sort by ratio
    res.sort(key=lambda x:x[3])
    return res[:20]
possible_pairs()

def min_divisor_for_n(c, multM=1):
    # n = M*multM + c, where multM is integer multiplier of M (1 for M, 5 for 5M)
    # c is integer (could be 0)
    # return smallest m >=7 dividing n such that (m-1) not prime
    # We'll search m up to some bound, maybe 2000, but should find small.
    for m in range(7, 5000):  # search up to 5000
        # compute n mod m
        # compute M mod m
        if m % 3 == 0:
            # M is power of 3, exponent >= log3(m) so M mod m = 0
            M_mod = 0
        else:
            # compute 3^A mod m, with exponent A huge but A mod phi(m) = 0 because A multiple of phi(m)
            # So M_mod = 1
            M_mod = 1
        # n = multM * M + c
        n_mod = (multM * M_mod + c) % m
        if n_mod == 0:
            # check (m-1) composite
            if not sp.isprime(m-1):
                return m
    return None

# test for each case
c_cases = [0, 0, 1848374, 10162574, 265710644, 44636594]  # c for each case, but for case 1 multM=5
multM_cases = [1,5,1,1,1,1]
for i,(c,mult) in enumerate(zip(c_cases,multM_cases)):
    m = min_divisor_for_n(c, mult)
    print(i, m)

from fractions import Fraction

def r_of_m(m):
    # compute smallest divisor b >=3 of m-1
    n = m-1
    # find smallest divisor >=3
    b = None
    for d in range(3, int(math.isqrt(n))+1):
        if n % d == 0:
            b = d
            break
    if b is None:
        # n is prime? but then m not allowed. Should not happen.
        raise ValueError("m-1 prime")
    a = n // b
    # compute ratio = a*(b-1)/m
    return Fraction(a*(b-1), m)

for m in [9,25,97,103]:
    print(m, r_of_m(m))

def min_divisor_exact(c, multM=1, max_m=5000):
    for m in range(7, max_m+1):
        M_mod = pow(3, A, m)  # exact
        n_mod = (multM * M_mod + c) % m
        if n_mod == 0:
            if not sp.isprime(m-1):
                return m
    return None

[CODE] (turn 12):
def min_r_for_n(c, multM=1, max_m=5000):
    best = None
    best_m = None
    for m in range(7, max_m+1):
        if (multM * pow(3, A, m) + c) % m == 0:
            if not sp.isprime(m-1):
                r = r_of_m(m)
                if best is None or r < best:
                    best = r
                    best_m = m
    return best, best_m

for i, (c,mult) in enumerate(zip(c_vals,multM_vals)):
    r,bm = min_r_for_n(c,mult)
    print(i, bm, r)

[CODE] (turn 1):
import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, limit=10000):
    # search x from 1 upward
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        # get divisors
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # need three distinct positive divisors
        # choose any three distinct
        if len(divs) < 3:
            continue
        # check combos
        for a in range(len(divs)):
            for b in range(a+1, len(divs)):
                for c in range(b+1, len(divs)):
                    if divs[a] + divs[b] + divs[c] == n:
                        return x
    return None

def compute_f_vals(N):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        res[n] = f_bruteforce(n, limit=5000)
    return res

vals = compute_f_vals(200)
vals[:10]

import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, limit=10000):
    # search x from 1 upward
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        # get divisors
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # need three distinct positive divisors
        # choose any three distinct
        if len(divs) < 3:
            continue
        # check combos
        for a in range(len(divs)):
            for b in range(a+1, len(divs)):
                for c in range(b+1, len(divs)):
                    if divs[a] + divs[b] + divs[c] == n:
                        return x
    return None

def compute_f_vals(N):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        res[n] = f_bruteforce(n, limit=5000)
    return res

[CODE] (turn 6):
def f_using_1d(n):
    best = None
    for d in range(2, n):  # d>1
        x = n - d - 1
        if x <= d:
            continue
        if x % d == 0:
            if best is None or x < best:
                best = x
    return best

# compare for n up to 200
diff = []
for n in range(6, 201):
    f_br = f_bruteforce(n, limit=5000)
    f_form = f_using_1d(n)
    if f_br != f_form:
        diff.append((n, f_br, f_form))
len(diff), diff[:20]

def f_using_1d(n):
    best = None
    for d in range(2, n):  # d>1
        x = n - d - 1
        if x <= d:
            continue
        if x % d == 0:
            if best is None or x < best:
                best = x
    return best

# compare for n up to 200
diff = []
for n in range(6, 201):
    f_br = f_bruteforce(n, limit=5000)
    f_form = f_using_1d(n)
    if f_br != f_form:
        diff.append((n, f_br, f_form))

[CODE] (turn 7):
def find_triple(x, n):
    divs = sp.divisors(x)
    for i in range(len(divs)):
        for j in range(i+1, len(divs)):
            for k in range(j+1, len(divs)):
                if divs[i]+divs[j]+divs[k]==n:
                    return (divs[i], divs[j], divs[k])
    return None

def f_bruteforce_with_triple(n, limit=5000):
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        if len(divs) < 3:
            continue
        for i in range(len(divs)):
            for j in range(i+1, len(divs)):
                for k in range(j+1, len(divs)):
                    if divs[i]+divs[j]+divs[k]==n:
                        return x, (divs[i], divs[j], divs[k])
    return None

samples = {}
for n in range(6, 31):
    x,triple = f_bruteforce_with_triple(n, limit=5000)
    samples[n]=(x,triple)
samples

[CODE] (turn 7):
def find_triple(x, n):
    divs = sp.divisors(x)
    for i in range(len(divs)):
        for j in range(i+1, len(divs)):
            for k in range(j+1, len(divs)):
                if divs[i]+divs[j]+divs[k]==n:
                    return (divs[i], divs[j], divs[k])
    return None

def f_bruteforce_with_triple(n, limit=5000):
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        if len(divs) < 3:
            continue
        for i in range(len(divs)):
            for j in range(i+1, len(divs)):
                for k in range(j+1, len(divs)):
                    if divs[i]+divs[j]+divs[k]==n:
                        return x, (divs[i], divs[j], divs[k])
    return None

samples = {}
for n in range(6, 31):
    x,triple = f_bruteforce_with_triple(n, limit=5000)
    samples[n]=(x,triple)

def compute_f_bruteforce_up_to(N):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        res[n] = f_bruteforce(n, limit=5000)  # may need larger limit for larger n
    return res

# compute up to 200 maybe

[CODE] (turn 10):
def f_using_x_included(n, limit=5000):
    best = None
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        if x >= n:  # then n - x <=0 cannot be sum of positive divisors
            continue
        target = n - x
        # need two distinct proper divisors a,b of x (a<b) with a+b=target
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # proper divisors exclude x
        proper = [d for d in divs if d != x]
        # need distinct a,b
        s = set(proper)
        found=False
        for a in proper:
            b = target - a
            if b> a and b in s:  # distinct and >a
                found=True
                break
        if found:
            if best is None or x < best:
                best = x
    return best

diff2 = []
for n in range(6,201):
    f_br = vals[n]
    f_form = f_using_x_included(n, limit=5000)
    if f_br != f_form:
        diff2.append((n, f_br, f_form))
len(diff2), diff2[:20]

def f_using_x_included(n, limit=5000):
    best = None
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        if x >= n:  # then n - x <=0 cannot be sum of positive divisors
            continue
        target = n - x
        # need two distinct proper divisors a,b of x (a<b) with a+b=target
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # proper divisors exclude x
        proper = [d for d in divs if d != x]
        # need distinct a,b
        s = set(proper)
        found=False
        for a in proper:
            b = target - a
            if b> a and b in s:  # distinct and >a
                found=True
                break
        if found:
            if best is None or x < best:
                best = x
    return best

[CODE] (turn 11):
def f_bruteforce_fast(n, limit=5000):
    # use x-included method but also fallback to full search for small n
    x = f_using_x_included(n, limit=limit)
    if x is not None:
        return x
    # fallback to full search
    return f_bruteforce(n, limit=limit)

# compute ratio x/n for n up to 200
ratios = []
for n in range(6,201):
    x = f_bruteforce_fast(n, limit=5000)
    ratios.append(x/n)
max(ratios), min(ratios)

def f_bruteforce_fast_limit(n):
    # try x up to maybe n (since x cannot exceed n unless exceptional)
    # but we can search up to n+100 maybe
    limit = n+200
    return f_using_x_included(n, limit=limit) or f_bruteforce(n, limit=limit)

max_ratio = 0
max_n = None
for n in range(6,501):
    x = f_bruteforce_fast_limit(n)
    if x is None:
        continue
    ratio = x/n
    if ratio > max_ratio:
        max_ratio = ratio
        max_n = n
max_ratio, max_n

[CODE] (turn 13):
def f_via_smax(n, limit=5000):
    # compute s_max
    best_s = -1
    best_x = None
    for s in range(1, n):  # s positive
        x = n - s
        # need proper divisors of x distinct and sum to s
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        proper = [d for d in divs if d != x]
        # need two distinct proper divisors a,b with a+b=s
        found=False
        sset=set(proper)
        for a in proper:
            b = s - a
            if b> a and b in sset:
                found=True
                break
        if found:
            if s > best_s:
                best_s = s
                best_x = x
    return best_x

# test for n up to 200
diff3 = []
for n in range(6,201):
    x1 = vals[n]
    x2 = f_via_smax(n)
    if x1 != x2:
        diff3.append((n, x1, x2))
len(diff3), diff3[:20]

[CODE] (turn 16):
def largest_proper_divisor(n):
    # largest divisor less than n
    # if n is prime, largest proper divisor is 1
    # else n / smallest prime factor
    sp = sp.factorint(n)
    if len(sp)==1 and list(sp.values())[0]==1:
        return 1
    # smallest prime factor
    for p in sp:
        return n // p  # first key is smallest prime factor (dict order not guaranteed)
    # fallback
    return 1

def compare_lp(n):
    return largest_proper_divisor(n)

diff_lp = []
for n in range(6,101):
    smax = n - vals[n]
    lp = compare_lp(n)
    if smax != lp:
        diff_lp.append((n, smax, lp))
len(diff_lp), diff_lp[:20]

[CODE] (turn 17):
import sympy as sp
def largest_proper_divisor(n):
    fac = sp.factorint(n)
    if n==1:
        return 0
    # if n is prime, largest proper divisor is 1
    if len(fac)==1 and list(fac.values())[0]==1:
        return 1
    # smallest prime factor
    spf = min(fac.keys())
    return n // spf

diff_lp = []
for n in range(6,101):
    smax = n - vals[n]
    lp = largest_proper_divisor(n)
    if smax != lp:
        diff_lp.append((n, smax, lp))
len(diff_lp), diff_lp[:20]

[CODE] (turn 23):
def f_formula_odd(n):
    # n odd >=7
    assert n%2==1
    # compute even divisors of n-1
    N = n-1
    # find largest even divisor d < N such that d != N/2 (if N/2 even)
    # Actually we need d even and d < N
    # We'll iterate over divisors
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    # filter even and less than N
    evens = [d for d in divs if d%2==0 and d < N]
    if not evens:
        return None
    # sort descending
    evens.sort(reverse=True)
    for d in evens:
        # check not equal to N/2 (if that would make x = d)
        if d == N//2 and N%2==0:
            # if N/2 is even? Actually N even always; but we need to avoid case where x = d
            # x = n - 1 - d = N - d
            # if d == N/2 => x = N - N/2 = N/2 = d, not allowed
            continue
        x = n - 1 - d
        # ensure x > d? Actually need x > d? Since d must be proper divisor of x, we need d < x.
        # x = N - d, and d < N, so x > 0. Need d < x => d < N - d => 2d < N => d < N/2.
        # So we also need d < N/2 to ensure d is proper divisor (since x = N - d, and d divides x? Wait we need d | x.
        # Our construction ensures d | x because d | N and x = N - d = N - d = N - d = N - d.
        # Since d | N, N = d * k. Then x = d*k - d = d*(k-1). So d divides x.
        # Need d != x => k-1 != 1 => k != 2 => N != 2d (i.e., d != N/2). Already avoided.
        # Also need x > d => d*(k-1) > d => k-1 > 1 => k > 2 => N > 2d. So we need N > 2d.
        # So we need d < N/2.
        if d < N/2:
            return x
    return None

# test for odd n up to 200
mismatch = []
for n in range(7,201,2):
    f_br = odd_vals[n]
    f_form = f_formula_odd(n)
    if f_br != f_form:
        mismatch.append((n, f_br, f_form))
len(mismatch), mismatch[:20]

[CODE] (turn 23):
def f_formula_odd(n):
    # n odd >=7
    assert n%2==1
    # compute even divisors of n-1
    N = n-1
    # find largest even divisor d < N such that d != N/2 (if N/2 even)
    # Actually we need d even and d < N
    # We'll iterate over divisors
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    # filter even and less than N
    evens = [d for d in divs if d%2==0 and d < N]
    if not evens:
        return None
    # sort descending
    evens.sort(reverse=True)
    for d in evens:
        # check not equal to N/2 (if that would make x = d)
        if d == N//2 and N%2==0:
            # if N/2 is even? Actually N even always; but we need to avoid case where x = d
            # x = n - 1 - d = N - d
            # if d == N/2 => x = N - N/2 = N/2 = d, not allowed
            continue
        x = n - 1 - d
        # ensure x > d? Actually need x > d? Since d must be proper divisor of x, we need d < x.
        # x = N - d, and d < N, so x > 0. Need d < x => d < N - d => 2d < N => d < N/2.
        # So we also need d < N/2 to ensure d is proper divisor (since x = N - d, and d divides x? Wait we need d | x.
        # Our construction ensures d | x because d | N and x = N - d = N - d = N - d = N - d.
        # Since d | N, N = d * k. Then x = d*k - d = d*(k-1). So d divides x.
        # Need d != x => k-1 != 1 => k != 2 => N != 2d (i.e., d != N/2). Already avoided.
        # Also need x > d => d*(k-1) > d => k-1 > 1 => k > 2 => N > 2d. So we need N > 2d.
        # So we need d < N/2.
        if d < N/2:
            return x
    return None

# test for odd n up to 200
mismatch = []
for n in range(7,201,2):
    f_br = odd_vals[n]
    f_form = f_formula_odd(n)
    if f_br != f_form:
        mismatch.append((n, f_br, f_form))
len(mismatch), mismatch[:20]

[CODE] (turn 33):
def min_even_x_for_s(s, limit=1000):
    # find smallest even x such that there exist distinct proper divisors a,b of x with a+b = s
    for x in range(2, limit+1, 2):
        # proper divisors
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        proper = [d for d in divs if d != x]
        # need distinct a,b
        sset = set(proper)
        for a in proper:
            b = s - a
            if b > a and b in sset:
                return x
    return None

min_x_for_s = {s: min_even_x_for_s(s, limit=2000) for s in range(3,101,2)}
min_x_for_s[:10]

[CODE] (turn 36):
def f_bruteforce_limit(n, limit_factor=2):
    # search x from n//2+1 upward (since x > n/2)
    start = n//2 + 1
    for x in range(start, n):
        if x%2!=0:
            continue
        # need two proper divisors sum to n - x
        s = n - x
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        proper = [d for d in divs if d != x]
        sset = set(proper)
        for a in proper:
            b = s - a
            if b > a and b in sset:
                return x
    return None

def f_fast(n):
    return f_bruteforce_limit(n)

for n in [101, 1001, 10001, 100001]:
    print(n, f_fast(n))

def f_bruteforce_limit(n, limit_factor=2):
    # search x from n//2+1 upward (since x > n/2)
    start = n//2 + 1
    for x in range(start, n):
        if x%2!=0:
            continue
        # need two proper divisors sum to n - x
        s = n - x
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        proper = [d for d in divs if d != x]
        sset = set(proper)
        for a in proper:
            b = s - a
            if b > a and b in sset:
                return x
    return None

[CODE] (turn 40):
def compute_s_via_eq(n):
    # solve s = odd_part((n - s)//2) + 2, with s odd, 1 <= s < n/2
    for s in range((n//2)-1, 0, -2):  # descending odd s
        t = (n - s)//2
        # compute odd part
        odd = t // (2**sp.valuation(t, 2))
        if s == odd + 2:
            return s
    return None

for n in [101, 103, 105, 107, 109]:
    print(n, compute_s_via_eq(n))

[CODE] (turn 1):
import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, limit=10000):
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        # get divisors
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # need three distinct positive divisors
        # choose combos
        for a in range(len(divs)):
            for b in range(a+1, len(divs)):
                for c in range(b+1, len(divs)):
                    if divs[a] + divs[b] + divs[c] == n:
                        return x
    return None

def compute_f_up_to(N):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        res[n] = f_bruteforce(n, limit=2000)
    return res

res = compute_f_up_to(100)
res[:10]

import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, limit=10000):
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        # get divisors
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # need three distinct positive divisors

        for a in range(len(divs)):

                for c in range(b+1, len(divs)):

                        return x
    return None

[CODE] (turn 7):
def f_exact(n):
    # search up to maybe n*2 or something
    limit = max(1000, 2*n)  # heuristics
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # need 3 distinct divisors
        L = len(divs)
        # early break if sum of smallest three > n? Not needed.
        for i in range(L):
            for j in range(i+1, L):
                for k in range(j+1, L):
                    if divs[i] + divs[j] + divs[k] == n:
                        return x
    return None

# test for n up to 200
vals = [f_exact(n) for n in range(6, 201)]
for n in range(6, 31):
    print(n, vals[n-6])

    # search up to maybe n*2 or something
    limit = max(1000, 2*n)  # heuristics
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        # need 3 distinct divisors

            for j in range(i+1, L):

[CODE] (turn 8):
def f_bruteforce_limit(n, max_x=5000):
    # compute f(n) using brute force up to max_x
    for x in range(1, max_x+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        L = len(divs)
        for i in range(L):
            for j in range(i+1, L):
                for k in range(j+1, L):
                    if divs[i] + divs[j] + divs[k] == n:
                        return x
    return None

def f_small(n):
    # search up to maybe 5*n
    return f_bruteforce_limit(n, max_x=5*n)

def test_M(k, max_c=200):
    M = 3**k
    results = {}
    for c in range(0, max_c+1):
        N = M + c
        fN = f_small(N)
        results[c] = fN
    return results

for k in range(1,6):
    M = 3**k
    res = test_M(k, max_c=50)
    print("k",k,"M",M)
    for c in range(0, 11):
        print(c, res[c])
    print()

[CODE] (turn 10):
def f_bruteforce_limit2(n, max_x):
    for x in range(1, max_x+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(x)
        L=len(divs)
        for i in range(L):
            for j in range(i+1, L):
                for k in range(j+1, L):
                    if divs[i]+divs[j]+divs[k]==n:
                        return x
    return None

def max_f_exceed(limit_n):
    for n in range(6, limit_n+1):
        f_val = f_bruteforce_limit2(n, max_x=2*n) # search up to 2n
        if f_val is None:
            print("not found for", n)
            return None
        if f_val > n:
            return n, f_val
    return None

max_f_exceed(500)

[CODE] (turn 15):
def find_triple(x, N):
    divs = sp.divisors(x)
    L=len(divs)
    for i in range(L):
        for j in range(i+1, L):
            for k in range(j+1, L):
                if divs[i]+divs[j]+divs[k]==N:
                    return (divs[i], divs[j], divs[k])
    return None

print(find_triple(18,27))

[CODE] (turn 20):
def is_sum_two_distinct_powers_of_3(c):
    powers = []
    i=0
    while 3**i <= c:
        powers.append(3**i)
        i+=1
    # check distinct
    for i in range(len(powers)):
        for j in range(i+1, len(powers)):
            if powers[i] + powers[j] == c:
                return True, (powers[i], powers[j])
    return False, None

c_vals = [1848374, 10162574, 265710644, 44636594]
for c in c_vals:
    ok, pair = is_sum_two_distinct_powers_of_3(c)
    print(c, ok, pair)

def f_M_plus_c_small(k, c):
    M = 3**k
    N = M + c
    fN = f_bruteforce_limit3(N, max_x=10*M)  # search up to 10M
    return fN

def t_factor(k,c):
    M = 3**k
    fN = f_M_plus_c_small(k,c)
    if fN % M == 0:
        return fN // M
    else:
        return None

k=5
M=3**k
for c in range(0,21):
    t = t_factor(k,c)
    print(c, f_M_plus_c_small(k,c), t)

[REASONING] (turn 22):
\[
M=3^{2025!},\qquad 
g(c)=\frac{1}{2025!}\Bigl\lfloor\frac{2025!\,f(M+c)}{M}\Bigr\rfloor .
\]

--------------------------------------------------------------------
### 1.  A reformulation of \(f(n)\)

A positive integer \(x\) is \(n\)-Norwegian iff there are three distinct
positive divisors \(a,b,c\mid x\) with  

\[
a+b+c=n .
\]

For a fixed triple \((a,b,c)\) the smallest integer having them as divisors
is \(\operatorname{lcm}(a,b,c)\); any integer possessing the three
divisors must be a multiple of this l.c.m.  
Consequently  

\[
\boxed{\,f(n)=\min_{\substack{a,b,c\in\mathbb Z_{>0}\\ a\neq b\neq c\\
a+b+c=n}}\operatorname{lcm}(a,b,c)} .
\tag{1}
\]

--------------------------------------------------------------------
### 2.  Two special values

*For a pure power of three.*  
Let \(M=3^{K}\;(K\ge 2)\).  
Choosing the triple \((3^{K-2},\,3^{K-1},\,3^{K})\) we obtain  

\[
\operatorname{lcm}=3^{K-1}\cdot 2 =\frac{2}{3}M .
\]

No smaller l.c.m. can give the sum \(M\); hence  

\[
\boxed{f(M)=\frac23M}. \tag{2}
\]

*For five times a power of three.*  
Let \(N=5M=5\cdot 3^{K}\).  
The triple  

\[
\bigl(5\cdot 3^{K-2},\;10\cdot 3^{K-2},\;30\cdot 3^{K-2}\bigr)
\]

consists of distinct divisors of  

\[
x=10\cdot 3^{K-1}=\frac{10}{3}M,
\]

and their sum is  

\[
(5+10+30)3^{K-2}=45\cdot 3^{K-2}=5\cdot 3^{K}=N .
\]

Again this is the smallest possible l.c.m., so  

\[
\boxed{f(5M)=\frac{10}{3}M}. \tag{3}
\]

--------------------------------------------------------------------
### 3.  Behaviour of \(g(c)\)

Write \(f(M+c)=\dfrac{a}{3^{K}}\) with \(a\in\mathbb Z_{>0}\).
Then  

\[
g(c)=\frac{1}{2025!}\Bigl\lfloor\frac{2025!\,a}{3^{K}}\Bigr\rfloor .
\tag{4}
\]

The exponent of the prime \(3\) in \(2025!\) is  

\[
v_{3}(2025!)=\sum_{i\ge1}\Bigl\lfloor\frac{2025}{3^{i}}\Bigr\rfloor =1010 .
\]

Hence \(2025!\) is divisible by \(3^{1010}\) but by no higher power of
\(3\).

If \(v_{3}(a)\le K-1011\) then \(3^{K}\) does **not** divide
\(2025!\,a\); the fraction in (4) is less than \(1\), so the floor is
\(0\) and consequently \(g(c)=0\).

For the two values already computed we have  

* \(c=0\): by (2) \(a=\frac23M=2\cdot3^{K-1}\), so \(v_{3}(a)=K-1\ge K-1010\);
  the denominator \(3\) cancels, and  

  \[
  g(0)=\frac{2025!}{3}\cdot\frac{2}{2025!}= \frac23 .
  \]

* \(c=4M\): by (3) \(a=\frac{10}{3}M=10\cdot3^{K-1}\), again
  \(v_{3}(a)=K-1\); the denominator \(3\) cancels and  

  \[
  g(4M)=\frac{10}{3}.
  \]

For the remaining five values of \(c\) we have \(0<c<3^{K-1}\); none of
them is a sum of two distinct powers of \(3\).  Consequently any
triple of divisors giving the sum \(M+c\) must contain a divisor whose
\(3\)-adic valuation is at most \(K-2\), whence  

\[
v_{3}\bigl(f(M+c)\bigr)\le K-2<K-1010 .
\]

Thus for each of those five \(c\) we obtain \(g(c)=0\).

--------------------------------------------------------------------
### 4.  The required sum

\[
\begin{aligned}
\sum g(c) &=
g(0)+g(4M)+g(1848374)+g(10162574)+g(265710644)+g(44636594)\\[2mm]
&= \frac23+\frac{10}{3}+0+0+0+0 = 4 .
\end{aligned}
\]

Hence  

\[
\frac{p}{q}=4=\frac{4}{1},\qquad p+q=5 .
\]

Finally  

\[
\boxed{5\pmod{99991}=5}.
\]

[CODE] (turn 1):
import sympy as sp

def f_brutal(N):
    S = N-1
    best = None
    best_pair = None
    for a in range(2, S):
        b = S - a
        if b <= a: continue  # ensure a<b distinct
        # compute lcm
        l = sp.ilcm(a,b)
        if best is None or l < best:
            best = l
            best_pair = (a,b)
    return best, best_pair

def f_formula(N):
    S = N-1
    p = sp.factorint(S)
    # smallest prime factor
    spf = min(p.keys())
    return S - spf

def test(limit):
    for N in range(6, limit+1):
        bf, pair = f_brutal(N)
        ff = f_formula(N)
        if bf != ff:
            print(N, bf, ff, pair)
            break
    else:
        print("All equal up to", limit)

test(200)

[CODE] (turn 2):
def test_composite(limit):
    for N in range(6, limit+1):
        S = N-1
        if sp.isprime(S):
            continue
        bf,_ = f_brutal(N)
        ff = S - min(sp.factorint(S).keys())
        if bf != ff:
            print("Mismatch at", N, bf, ff)
            break
    else:
        print("All composite up to", limit, "match")
test_composite(200)

[CODE] (turn 8):
def smallest_divisor_gt2_of_S(c):
    # returns smallest integer d>2 dividing S = M + c -1
    # We'll search up to some limit, but likely small.
    for d in range(3, 1000):  # search up to 1000
        # compute M mod d
        m_mod = M_mod(d)
        # compute S mod d
        s_mod = (m_mod + (c - 1) % d) % d
        if s_mod == 0:
            return d
    return None

c_vals = [0, 4*fact2025, 1848374, 10162574, 265710644, 44636594]
for c in c_vals:
    print(c, smallest_divisor_gt2_of_S(c))

[CODE] (turn 1):
def f_bruteforce(n, limit=1000):
    best = None
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, n-1):
        for b in range(a+1, n):
            c = n - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            # distinct positive
            import math
            l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
                best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

def compute_f_up_to(N):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        best,_ = f_bruteforce(n)
        res[n]=best
    return res

print(f_bruteforce(6))
print(f_bruteforce(7))
print(f_bruteforce(8))
print(f_bruteforce(9))
print(f_bruteforce(10))

[CODE] (turn 1):
def f_bruteforce(n, limit=1000):
    best = None
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, n-1):
        for b in range(a+1, n):
            c = n - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            # distinct positive
            import math
            l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
                best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

def compute_f_up_to(N):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        best,_ = f_bruteforce(n)
        res[n]=best
    return res

[CODE] (turn 4):
import math, itertools

def min_ratio_for_sum(S):
    best = None
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, S-1):
        for b in range(a+1, S):
            c = S - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            L = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            ratio = L / S
            if best is None or ratio < best - 1e-12:
                best = ratio
                best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

powers = [3**k for k in range(2,8)]  # up to 3^7=2187
for S in powers:
    r, trip = min_ratio_for_sum(S)
    print(S, r, trip)

import math, itertools

def min_ratio_for_sum(S):
    best = None
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, S-1):
        for b in range(a+1, S):
            c = S - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            L = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            ratio = L / S
            if best is None or ratio < best - 1e-12:
                best = ratio
                best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

powers = [3**k for k in range(2,8)]  # up to 3^7=2187
for S in powers:
    r, trip = min_ratio_for_sum(S)
    print(S, r, trip)

[CODE] (turn 5):
def min_ratio_for_sum(S):
    best = None
    for a in range(1, S-1):
        for b in range(a+1, S):
            c = S - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            L = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            ratio = L / S
            if best is None or ratio < best:
                best = ratio
    return best

def min_ratio_for_allowed(N_factors):
    # N_factors: set of allowed prime factors (odd)
    best = None
    best_S = None
    # search S up to some limit
    for S in range(6, 500):
        if S%2==0: continue
        # check if S's prime factors subset of allowed
        # factor S
        temp=S
        ok=True
        for p in [3,5]:
            while temp%p==0:
                temp//=p
        if temp!=1: ok=False
        if not ok: continue
        # S divides N (since N has unlimited powers of 3 and at most one 5)
        # need to ensure exponent of 5 in S <=1
        # Already accounted.
        r = min_ratio_for_sum(S)
        if best is None or r < best:
            best=r
            best_S=S
    return best, best_S

print(min_ratio_for_allowed({3,5}))

[CODE] (turn 5):
def min_ratio_for_sum(S):
    best = None
    for a in range(1, S-1):
        for b in range(a+1, S):
            c = S - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            L = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            ratio = L / S
            if best is None or ratio < best:
                best = ratio
    return best

def min_ratio_for_allowed(N_factors):
    # N_factors: set of allowed prime factors (odd)
    best = None
    best_S = None
    # search S up to some limit
    for S in range(6, 500):
        if S%2==0: continue
        # check if S's prime factors subset of allowed
        # factor S
        temp=S
        ok=True
        for p in [3,5]:
            while temp%p==0:
                temp//=p
        if temp!=1: ok=False
        if not ok: continue
        # S divides N (since N has unlimited powers of 3 and at most one 5)
        # need to ensure exponent of 5 in S <=1
        # Already accounted.
        r = min_ratio_for_sum(S)
        if best is None or r < best:
            best=r
            best_S=S
    return best, best_S

print(min_ratio_for_allowed({3,5}))

def best_trip_for_sum(S):
    best = None
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, S-1):
        for b in range(a+1, S):
            c = S - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            L = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            ratio = L / S
            if best is None or ratio < best:
                best = ratio
                best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

print(best_trip_for_sum(25))

def min_ratio_bruteforce(S):

    best_trip = None

            if c <= b: continue

def compute_ratios(limit):
    data = {}
    for S in range(6, limit+1):
        if S%2==0: continue  # only odd? Actually we can compute all.
        r,trip = min_ratio_bruteforce(S)
        data[S]=(r,trip)
    return data

def min_ratio_up_to(limit):

        if S%2==0: continue

        if r < best - 1e-12:
            best = r
            best_S = S
            best_trip = trip
    return best, best_S, best_trip

def find_better(limit):
    best = 6/11
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, limit):
        for b in range(a+1, limit):
            for c in range(b+1, limit):
                S = a+b+c
                L = math.lcm(a,b,c)
                r = L/S
                if r < best - 1e-12:
                    best = r
                    best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

# This brute force will be huge; limit maybe 50
print(find_better(50))

[CODE] (turn 21):
def find_ratio(target, limit=200):
    for S in range(6, limit+1):
        for a in range(1, S-1):
            for b in range(a+1, S):
                c = S - a - b
                if c <= b: continue
                L = math.lcm(a,b,c)
                if L * target[1] == S * target[0]:
                    return (S, (a,b,c), L)
    return None

print(find_ratio((5,9), 200))

def odd_non3_part(n):
    # remove all factors of 2 and 3
    while n%2==0:
        n//=2
    while n%3==0:
        n//=3
    return n

for c in c_vals:
    n = c+1
    m = odd_non3_part(n)
    print(c, n, m, sp.factorint(m))

def precompute_r0(limit):

    for S in range(6, limit+1):

        for a in range(1, S-1):
            for b in range(a+1, S):

                if c <= b: continue
                L = math.lcm(a,b,c)

    return r0

import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, max_m=None):
    if max_m is None:
        max_m = n*2  # search up to 2n maybe
    for m in range(1, max_m+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(m)
        # need three distinct divisors sum to n
        # iterate combos
        L = len(divs)
        for i in range(L):
            for j in range(i+1, L):
                for k in range(j+1, L):
                    if divs[i] + divs[j] + divs[k] == n:
                        return m
    return None

# compute for n from 6 to 30
results = {}
for n in range(6, 31):
    results[n] = f_bruteforce(n, max_m=200)  # maybe enough
results

def f_bruteforce_limit(n, limit_factor=3):
    # search up to limit_factor*n

    for m in range(1, max_m+1):

    return None

[CODE] (turn 7):
import sympy as sp

def f_candidate(n):
    # compute smallest m via construction using divisor 1
    # find largest proper divisor k of n-1 not equal to (n-1)/2
    N = n-1
    # get divisors of N
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    # proper divisors >1 and <N
    candidates = [k for k in divs if 1 < k < N and k != N//2]  # N//2 only if N even
    if not candidates:
        return None
    k = max(candidates)  # largest proper divisor meeting condition
    m = n - k - 1
    return m

def f_bruteforce_small(n, limit=500):
    for m in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(m)
        L = len(divs)
        for i in range(L):
            for j in range(i+1, L):
                for k in range(j+1, L):
                    if divs[i] + divs[j] + divs[k] == n:
                        return m
    return None

# test for n up to 200
mismatch = []
for n in range(6, 201):
    f1 = f_bruteforce_small(n, limit=500)
    f2 = f_candidate(n)

[CODE] (turn 7):
import sympy as sp

def f_candidate(n):
    # compute smallest m via construction using divisor 1
    # find largest proper divisor k of n-1 not equal to (n-1)/2
    N = n-1
    # get divisors of N
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    # proper divisors >1 and <N
    candidates = [k for k in divs if 1 < k < N and k != N//2]  # N//2 only if N even
    if not candidates:
        return None
    k = max(candidates)  # largest proper divisor meeting condition
    m = n - k - 1
    return m

def f_bruteforce_small(n, limit=500):
    for m in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(m)
        L = len(divs)
        for i in range(L):
            for j in range(i+1, L):
                for k in range(j+1, L):
                    if divs[i] + divs[j] + divs[k] == n:
                        return m
    return None

# test for n up to 200
mismatch = []
for n in range(6, 201):
    f1 = f_bruteforce_small(n, limit=500)
    f2 = f_candidate(n)
    if f1 != f2:
        mismatch.append((n, f1, f2))
len(mismatch), mismatch[:20]

def f_bruteforce_limit2(n, max_m=None):

    return None

def f_bruteforce_up_to(n, max_m_factor=2):
    # compute f(n) by brute up to max_m_factor*n
    max_m = int(max_m_factor * n)
    return f_bruteforce_limit2(n, max_m=max_m)

[CODE] (turn 18):
def v_p_factorial(n, p):
    cnt = 0
    power = p
    while power <= n:
        cnt += n // power
        power *= p
    return cnt

v3 = v_p_factorial(2025, 3)
v3

[CODE] (turn 18):
def v_p_factorial(n, p):
    cnt = 0
    power = p
    while power <= n:
        cnt += n // power
        power *= p
    return cnt

def f_bruteforce_limit_general(n, max_m):
    # brute search up to max_m
    for m in range(1, max_m+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(m)
        L = len(divs)
        for i in range(L):
            for j in range(i+1, L):
                for k in range(j+1, L):
                    if divs[i] + divs[j] + divs[k] == n:
                        return m
    return None

[CODE] (turn 2):
def f_bruteforce(n):
    best = None
    best_triplet = None
    # iterate over distinct a<b<c maybe to avoid permutations
    for a in range(1, n-2):
        for b in range(a+1, n-a-1):
            c = n - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            l = sp.ilcm(a,b,c)  # sympy integer lcm
            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
                best_triplet = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_triplet

for n in range(6, 31):
    best, trip = f_bruteforce(n)
    print(n, best, trip)

                best_triplet = (a,b,c)

def f_bruteforce_opt(n):
    # using earlier function
    return f_bruteforce_fast(n)[0]

def test_odd(N):
    mismatches = []
    for n in range(7, N+1, 2):  # odd n >=7
        f = f_bruteforce_opt(n)
        if f != n-3:
            mismatches.append((n,f,n-3))
    return mismatches

    f_vals = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):

    return f_vals

[CODE] (turn 22):
def f_bruteforce_fast_limit(n):
    # limit search for c maybe up to n
    best = None
    for a in range(1, n-1):
        for b in range(a+1, n):
            c = n - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            l = sp.ilcm(a,b,c)
            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
    return best

def f_bruteforce_opt(n):
    return f_bruteforce_fast(n)[0]

print(f_bruteforce_opt(45))

def f_bruteforce_fast_limit(n):
    # limit search for c maybe up to n
    best = None
    for a in range(1, n-1):

            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
    return best

def f_bruteforce_opt2(n):
    # using earlier function but may be slow for n up to 1000
    best = None
    for a in range(1, n-2):
        for b in range(a+1, n-a-1):
            c = n - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            l = sp.ilcm(a,b,c)
            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
    return best

[CODE] (turn 27):
def find_triplet(n):
    best = None
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, n-2):
        for b in range(a+1, n-a-1):
            c = n - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            l = sp.ilcm(a,b,c)
            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
                best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

print(find_triplet(245))

[CODE] (turn 27):
def find_triplet(n):
    best = None
    best_trip = None
    for a in range(1, n-2):
        for b in range(a+1, n-a-1):
            c = n - a - b
            if c <= b: continue
            l = sp.ilcm(a,b,c)
            if best is None or l < best:
                best = l
                best_trip = (a,b,c)
    return best, best_trip

print(find_triplet(245))

[CODE] (turn 4):
def smallest_prime_factor_of_S(s):
    # s is integer added to M, i.e., S = M + s
    # returns smallest prime factor of S (int) or None if not found up to some limit
    # We'll test primes using sympy primerange
    for p in sp.primerange(2, 10000):  # test up to 10000 maybe enough
        M_mod = pow(3, fact2025, p)
        if (M_mod + s) % p == 0:
            return p
    return None

# test for small s values
print(smallest_prime_factor_of_S(0))

def smallest_prime_factor_of_S(s):
    # s is integer added to M, i.e., S = M + s
    # returns smallest prime factor of S (int) or None if not found up to some limit
    # We'll test primes using sympy primerange
    for p in sp.primerange(2, 10000):  # test up to 10000 maybe enough
        M_mod = pow(3, fact2025, p)
        if (M_mod + s) % p == 0:
            return p
    return None

# test for small s values
print(smallest_prime_factor_of_S(0))

[CODE] (turn 5):
def smallest_prime_factor_of_S_general(k, r):
    # c = k*M + r, where k integer >=0, r integer (could be negative)
    # S = (k+1)*M + r - 1
    for p in sp.primerange(2, 100000):  # up to 100k maybe enough
        M_mod = pow(3, fact2025, p)
        S_mod = ((k+1) * M_mod + (r - 1)) % p
        if S_mod == 0:
            return p
    return None

# test for c=0: k=0, r=0
print(smallest_prime_factor_of_S_general(0,0))

Since 2025! is divisible by 3 (since 3 ≤ 2025), 2025! / 3 is integer. So (2/3)*2025! = 2 * (2025!/3) is integer. Thus floor = same integer. So g(0) = (2 * (2025!/3)) / 2025! = 2/3.

Thus g(0) = 2/3.

General method: For given n = M + c, we can try to find N = k * 3^{L-1} where k is integer (maybe 2,3,4,...). Since divisors of N will be of form d = s * 3^{t} where s divides k and t ≤ L-1.

Goal: Choose N minimal such that there exist three distinct divisors summing to n.

We can think of constructing N = a * 3^{L-1} where a is small integer (maybe 2,3,4,...). Since M = 3 * 3^{L-1}. Actually M = 3^{L} = 3 * 3^{L-1}. So M = 3 * 3^{L-1}. Our candidate N = 2 * 3^{L-1} (i.e., 2/3 M). Next candidate N = 3 * 3^{L-1} = M. Next candidate N = 4 * 3^{L-1} = (4/3)M. Next N = 5 * 3^{L-1} = (5/3)M, etc.

Thus we can search for minimal integer a (≥2) such that there exist three distinct divisors of N = a * 3^{L-1} summing to M + c.

Divisors of N are of form d = d0 * 3^{t}, where d0 divides a and t ∈ [0, L-1].

Since L huge, we can treat 3^{t} as huge scaling factor. So essentially the divisors are scaled copies of divisors of a multiplied by powers of 3.

Thus we can think of representing n = M + c = 3^{L-1} * 3 + c = 3^{L-1}*3 + c.

Let X = 3^{L-1} (a huge base). Then M = 3X. N = aX.

Divisors of N are of form (d0) * 3^{t}, with d0|a and t ≤ L-1. So each divisor can be expressed as (d0) * 3^{t} = (d0) * 3^{t}.

We need three distinct such divisors summing to 3X + c.

Since X is huge, we can think of representing the sum as combination of terms with possibly different t.

Observation: The three divisors can be expressed as:

d1 = u1 * 3^{t1}
d2 = u2 * 3^{t2}
d3 = u3 * 3^{t3}

where each ui divides a, each ti ∈ [0, L-1], and the three are distinct.

We can factor out the smallest power of 3 among them, say let t_min = min(ti). Then we can write each as 3^{t_min} * (ui * 3^{ti - t_min}). Since 3^{ti - t_min} is a power of 3.

Thus the sum is 3^{t_min} * (something). Since X = 3^{L-1} is huge, we can consider t_min possibly L-1 or less.

But we need sum to be 3X + c, where X = 3^{L-1}. Since X is a power of 3, we can try to align the largest power of 3 factor.

If we choose t_min = L-1 (i.e., all three divisors have factor 3^{L-1}), then each divisor is of form (ui) * 3^{L-1} where ui divides a. So each divisor is a multiple of X.

Then sum = X * (u1 + u2 + u3) = 3X + c.

Thus we need u1 + u2 + u3 = 3 + c/X.

Since c << X, c/X is a fraction less than 1. Since u_i are integers, sum must be integer. So we need u1+u2+u3 = 3, and the leftover c must be accounted by lower powers of 3 (i.e., using some divisors with t < L-1). So at least one divisor must have t < L-1 to contribute the small c.

Thus we can have two divisors with t = L-1 (i.e., multiples of X), and one divisor with lower power to provide the c offset.

Thus we can set:

d1 = a1 * X (where a1 divides a)
d2 = a2 * X (a2 divides a)
d3 = b * 3^{t} where t < L-1, b divides a.

Sum = X*(a1 + a2) + b*3^{t} = 3X + c.

Thus we need X*(a1 + a2 - 3) + b*3^{t} = c.

Since X is huge, the term X*(a1 + a2 - 3) must be zero, otherwise magnitude >> c. Therefore a1 + a2 = 3.

Thus we need two divisors of N that are multiples of X with coefficients a1,a2 (positive integers dividing a) such that a1 + a2 = 3, and a1 ≠ a2 (since divisors must be distinct). Also a1,a2 must be distinct from each other and from the third divisor.

Then the remaining small term b*3^{t} must equal c.

Thus we need to represent c as b*3^{t} where b divides a and t < L-1 (i.e., any non-negative integer). Since c is small, we can simply require c = b * 3^{t} with b|a.

Thus for given a (candidate multiplier), we need:

- Find distinct positive integers a1,a2 dividing a such that a1 + a2 = 3.
- Then need c = b * 3^{t} where b divides a and (b, t) not equal to a1*X or a2*X (i.e., distinct). Since a1*X and a2*X are huge, distinctness is automatic as long as b*3^{t} is not equal to those.

Thus the condition reduces to existence of a divisor a of which there are two distinct divisors a1,a2 summing to 3. Since a1,a2 are positive integers dividing a.

What possibilities for a? Since a1 + a2 = 3, possibilities (a1,a2) = (1,2) or (2,1). So we need a to be divisible by both 1 and 2, i.e., a must be even (multiple of 2). So a must be at least 2.

Thus minimal a = 2. Indeed a=2 gives divisors 1 and 2, sum 3. So a=2 works. That's our earlier N = 2X = (2/3)M.

Thus with a=2, we have a1=1, a2=2 (or vice versa). So the two large divisors are X (since a1=1) and 2X (i.e., N). Wait N = aX = 2X. So a2=2 gives divisor N itself (2X). So the two large divisors are X (i.e., N/2) and N (2X). They are distinct.

Now we need c = b * 3^{t} where b divides a (=2) and t < L-1. So b can be 1 or 2. So c must be either a power of 3 (b=1) or twice a power of 3 (b=2). That's exactly the condition earlier.

Thus for c that is a power of 3 or twice a power of 3, we can achieve with N = (2/3)M.

If c does not satisfy that, then we need to consider larger a.

Next candidate a = 3? Let's test.

a=3 => N = 3X = M. Divisors of a are 1,3. Need two distinct divisors a1,a2 dividing a with sum 3. Options: (1,2) not possible since 2 not divisor. (1,? ) only 1+?=3 => ?=2 not divisor. (3,0) not allowed. So no pair of distinct divisors sum to 3. So a=3 fails.

Next a=4: divisors: 1,2,4. Need two distinct divisors summing to 3: 1+2=3 works. So a=4 works. Then N = 4X = (4/3)M.

Now b must divide a=4, so b ∈ {1,2,4}. Need c = b * 3^{t}. So c must be either a power of 3 (b=1), twice a power of 3 (b=2), or four times a power of 3 (b=4). Since c is small, we can test.

Thus for c not representable as 1*3^{t} or 2*3^{t}, maybe 4*3^{t} works.

If still not, we can increase a.

Thus the minimal a such that c can be expressed as b*3^{t} with b|a, and also a has two distinct divisors summing to 3 (i.e., a must have divisors 1 and 2). So a must be even (multiple of 2). Also need a to have a divisor 2 (since need a1=1, a2=2). So a must be multiple of 2. That's sufficient: any even a will have divisors 1 and 2, sum 3.

Thus the condition reduces to: Find smallest even a such that c is of the form b * 3^{t} where b divides a.

Thus we need to factor c as (some divisor of a) times a power of 3.

Since a can be any even integer, we can always choose a = 2 * (c_without_3_power) maybe.

Let’s write c = d * 3^{t} where d is not divisible by 3 (i.e., the 3-free part). Then we need a even and divisible by d (since b must divide a). So we can choose a = 2d (if d even? Actually a must be even; 2d is even regardless). Then b can be d (if d divides a) or 2d etc.

Thus minimal even a that is multiple of d is a = 2d if d is odd (since need even). If d already even, then a = d (if d even) maybe? But need a even; if d even, a = d works (since d even). However also need a ≥ 2 (obviously). So minimal a = max(2, smallest even multiple of d). That's essentially a = 2 * d' where d' is d with maybe factor 2 accounted.

Thus f(M + c) = a * X = a * 3^{L-1} = (a/3) * M.

Since X = M/3.

Thus f(M + c) = (a/3) * M.

But note a must be integer, and f must be integer. Since M divisible by 3, (a/3) * M = a * (M/3). Since M/3 = X integer. So f = a * X.

Thus the ratio f(M + c) / M = a / 3.

Thus g(c) = floor(2025! * (a/3)) / 2025! = (floor(2025! * a / 3))/2025!.

Since X is huge, we can treat the representation in terms of X.

Let’s denote N = aX.

Divisors of N are of form d = d0 * 3^{t}, where d0 divides a, 0 ≤ t ≤ L-1.

Goal: Find three distinct such divisors d1,d2,d3 with sum = 3X + c.

Since X is huge, the sum contributions from terms with t = L-1 dominate (they are multiples of X). Terms with lower t are negligible compared to X but may add up to c.

Thus we can think of representing 3X + c as sum of three terms each being (d_i0) * 3^{t_i}. The t_i may be L-1 or less.

Let’s denote contributions at level L-1: each term with t_i = L-1 contributes (d_i0) * X.

Let’s define S = sum over i where t_i = L-1 of d_i0. Then the total contribution from those terms is S * X.

The remaining terms (with t_i < L-1) contribute at most something less than X (since 3^{t_i} ≤ X/3). So they can sum up to at most 2 * (X/3) = 2X/3 if we have two such terms. So to get total 3X + c, we need S to be either 3 (if c is accounted by lower terms) or maybe 2 with remainder etc.

Case 1: Use two terms with t=L-1 and one term with lower t.

Then S = a1 + a2 (two coefficients) and the third term contributes b * 3^{t} where t<L-1.

Equation: (a1 + a2) X + b * 3^{t} = 3X + c.

Thus (a1 + a2 - 3) X = c - b * 3^{t}.

Since left side is multiple of X (huge), right side must be zero (since c and b*3^{t} are << X). Therefore a1 + a2 = 3 and c = b * 3^{t}.

Thus we recover earlier condition: need a1 + a2 = 3 (distinct divisors of a) and c must be representable as divisor of a times power of 3 (with exponent less than L-1). This works for small c.

Case 2: Use one term with t=L-1 and two terms with lower t.

Then equation: a1 X + b1 * 3^{t1} + b2 * 3^{t2} = 3X + c.

Thus (a1 - 3) X = c - (b1 * 3^{t1} + b2 * 3^{t2}).

Again left side huge unless a1 = 3. So a1 must be 3. Then we need c = b1 * 3^{t1} + b2 * 3^{t2}. So we need to represent c as sum of two distinct divisors of a (since each b_i divides a) times powers of 3 (with exponent less than L-1). Since c is small, we can set t1 = t2 = v3(c) maybe.

Thus we need a to have divisor 3 (i.e., a multiple of 3). Also need to have two distinct divisors b1,b2 dividing a such that b1*3^{t1} + b2*3^{t2} = c.

Since c may have factor 3^t, we can factor out 3^{t}.

Thus we need to find a minimal a such that:

- a is multiple of 3 (so divisor 3 exists)
- There exist distinct divisors b1,b2 of a such that b1*3^{t1} + b2*3^{t2} = c, with t1,t2 ≤ L-1.

Given L huge, we can set t1 = t2 = v3(c) (the exponent of 3 in c), and then need b1 + b2 = d where d is the 3-free part of c. So we need two distinct divisors of a summing to d.

Thus condition: a must be multiple of 3, and there exist distinct divisors of a summing to d (the 3-free part of c). This may allow smaller a than case 1.

Case 3: Use zero terms with t=L-1 (i.e., all three terms have lower t). Then sum of three terms each ≤ X/3, total ≤ X, cannot reach 3X + c. So impossible.

Thus the minimal a will be the smallest integer for which either case 1 or case 2 can be satisfied.

Case 1 requires a even (to have divisor 2) and a multiple of d (the 3-free part of c). Minimal a = smallest even multiple of d.

Case 2 requires a multiple of 3, and there exist two distinct divisors of a summing to d. This could allow smaller a if d can be expressed as sum of two divisors of a that are not necessarily 1 and 2.

Goal: Find minimal a satisfying either condition.

Thus we need to compute for each c the minimal a.

2) c1 = 4M (large). We'll compute later.

[CODE] (turn 1):
import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, maxN=5000):
    # search for smallest N that has three distinct divisors summing to n
    for N in range(1, maxN+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(N)
        # need three distinct positive divisors
        # choose combinations of 3
        for a,b,c in sp.utilities.iterables.combinations(divs, 3):
            if a+b+c == n:
                return N
    return None

# compute for n from 6 to maybe 30
vals = {}
for n in range(6, 31):
    vals[n] = f_bruteforce(n, maxN=5000)
vals

[CODE] (turn 4):
import itertools, sympy as sp
def sums_of_three(N):
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    sums = set()
    for a,b,c in itertools.combinations(divs,3):
        sums.add(a+b+c)
    return sorted(sums)

sums_of_three(12)

import itertools, sympy as sp
def sums_of_three(N):
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    sums = set()
    for a,b,c in itertools.combinations(divs,3):
        sums.add(a+b+c)
    return sorted(sums)

[CODE] (turn 5):
def f_candidate(n):
    best = None
    for d in range(2, n):  # d>1
        N = n - d - 1
        if N <= 0:
            continue
        if N % d == 0 and N != d:
            if best is None or N < best:
                best = N
    return best

# compare for n up to 200
diffs = {}
for n in range(6, 201):
    brute = f_bruteforce(n, maxN=5000)
    cand = f_candidate(n)
    if brute != cand:
        diffs[n] = (brute, cand)
len(diffs), list(diffs.items())[:20]

[CODE] (turn 6):
def f_upper_bound(n):
    # compute N = n - d - 1 where d is largest divisor of n-1 not equal to (n-1)/2
    N = n-1
    # get divisors of n-1
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    # exclude 1 and N
    proper = [d for d in divs if 1 < d < N]
    # sort descending
    proper.sort(reverse=True)
    for d in proper:
        if d != N//2:  # ensure not equal to (n-1)/2
            # compute candidate N
            cand = n - d - 1
            # ensure cand positive and cand % d ==0 automatically true because n-1 - d = cand
            if cand > 0 and cand % d == 0 and cand != d:
                return cand
    return None

# compare for n up to 200
diffs2 = {}
for n in range(6, 201):
    brute = f_bruteforce(n, maxN=5000)
    ub = f_upper_bound(n)
    if brute != ub:
        diffs2[n] = (brute, ub)
len(diffs2), list(diffs2.items())[:20]

def f_formula(n):
    N_minus1 = n-1
    # factor to get smallest odd prime factor if needed
    # get prime factors
    pf = sp.factorint(N_minus1)
    # sort primes
    primes = sorted(pf.keys())
    # smallest prime factor
    p = primes[0]
    if p != 2:
        # use p
        return (n-1)*(p-1)//p
    else:
        # p=2, need smallest odd prime factor
        odd_primes = [prime for prime in primes if prime != 2]
        if not odd_primes:
            # n-1 is power of 2
            # find largest divisor not equal to (n-1)/2
            # we can try using divisor (n-1)//4 if exponent >=2 else None
            # compute exponent of 2
            exp = pf[2]
            if exp >= 2:
                d = (n-1)//4
                return n - d - 1
            else:
                return None
        q = odd_primes[0]
        return (n-1)*(q-1)//q

[CODE] (turn 7):
def f_formula(n):
    N_minus1 = n-1
    # factor to get smallest odd prime factor if needed
    # get prime factors
    pf = sp.factorint(N_minus1)
    # sort primes
    primes = sorted(pf.keys())
    # smallest prime factor
    p = primes[0]
    if p != 2:
        # use p
        return (n-1)*(p-1)//p
    else:
        # p=2, need smallest odd prime factor
        odd_primes = [prime for prime in primes if prime != 2]
        if not odd_primes:
            # n-1 is power of 2
            # find largest divisor not equal to (n-1)/2
            # we can try using divisor (n-1)//4 if exponent >=2 else None
            # compute exponent of 2
            exp = pf[2]
            if exp >= 2:
                d = (n-1)//4
                return n - d - 1
            else:
                return None
        q = odd_primes[0]
        return (n-1)*(q-1)//q

# compare for n up to 200
diffs3 = {}
for n in range(6, 201):
    brute = f_bruteforce(n, maxN=5000)
    formula = f_formula(n)
    if brute != formula:
        diffs3[n] = (brute, formula)
len(diffs3), list(diffs3.items())[:30]

def f_formula2(n):
    N_minus1 = n-1
    # get proper divisors >1
    divs = sp.divisors(N_minus1)
    proper = [d for d in divs if d > 1 and d < N_minus1]
    # exclude d = N_minus1/2
    candidates = [d for d in proper if d != N_minus1//2]
    if not candidates:
        return None
    d = max(candidates)  # largest allowed divisor
    N = n - d - 1
    # ensure N % d == 0 (should hold)
    if N % d != 0:
        print("error")
    return N

def f_formula_smallest_odd_prime(n):
    N_minus1 = n-1
    # find smallest odd prime divisor
    # factor N_minus1
    pf = sp.factorint(N_minus1)
    odd_primes = [p for p in pf if p % 2 == 1]
    if not odd_primes:
        # N_minus1 is power of 2
        # then smallest odd prime divisor does not exist; need alternative
        # For power of 2, we can use divisor d = (N_minus1)//4 maybe
        # Let's handle separately later
        return None
    r = min(odd_primes)
    return (n-1)*(r-1)//r

def f_formula_composite(n):
    N_minus1 = n-1
    # check composite
    if sp.isprime(N_minus1):
        return None
    # find smallest odd prime divisor r (if N_minus1 even, still need odd)
    # get factorization
    pf = sp.factorint(N_minus1)
    odd_primes = sorted([p for p in pf if p % 2 == 1])
    if not odd_primes:
        # N_minus1 is power of 2
        # need smallest odd prime divisor of N_minus1? none.
        # Use divisor d = N_minus1 // 4 (if exponent >=2) else None
        exp = pf.get(2,0)
        if exp >= 2:
            d = N_minus1 // 4
            N = n - d - 1
            return N
        else:
            return None
    r = odd_primes[0]
    d = N_minus1 // r
    N = n - d - 1
    return N

    brute = f_bruteforce(n, maxN=5000)
    cand = f_formula_composite(n)
    if brute != cand:

[CODE] (turn 12):
def f_bruteforce_limit(n, maxN=100000):
    # find smallest N up to some bound (maybe n)
    for N in range(1, maxN+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(N)
        for a,b,c in itertools.combinations(divs,3):
            if a+b+c == n:
                return N
    return None

M_small = 30031
def smallest_odd_prime_factor(x):
    for p in sp.primerange(3, int(math.sqrt(x))+2):
        if x % p == 0:
            return p
    if x % 2 == 0:
        # ignore even
        pass
    # if x is odd prime
    if x > 2:
        return x
    return None

def f_formula_general(n):
    # compute smallest odd prime divisor of n-1
    Nminus1 = n-1
    # find smallest odd prime divisor
    # if Nminus1 even, we ignore 2 and find odd
    # search up to some limit
    for p in sp.primerange(3, 1000):
        if Nminus1 % p == 0:
            r = p
            break
    else:
        # fallback: if Nminus1 is odd prime > 1000
        if Nminus1 % 2 == 1:
            r = Nminus1
        else:
            r = None
    if r is None:
        return None
    return (n-1)*(r-1)//r

# test for some c values
for c in range(0, 20):
    n = M_small + c
    brute = f_bruteforce_limit(n, maxN=50000)
    formula = f_formula_general(n)
    print(c, brute, formula)

def find_triple(N, target):
    divs = sp.divisors(N)
    for a,b,c in itertools.combinations(divs,3):
        if a+b+c == target:
            return (a,b,c)
    return None

import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, limit=10000):
    # search k from 1 upwards
    for k in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(k)
        # need three distinct positive divisors sum to n
        # choose combinations of 3 distinct divisors
        for a in range(len(divs)):
            for b in range(a+1, len(divs)):
                for c in range(b+1, len(divs)):
                    if divs[a] + divs[b] + divs[c] == n:
                        return k
    return None

def compute_f_up_to(N):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        res[n] = f_bruteforce(n, limit=5000)
    return res

res = compute_f_up_to(100)
res[:10]

import sympy as sp

def f_bruteforce(n, limit=10000):
    # search k from 1 upwards
    for k in range(1, limit+1):
        divs = sp.divisors(k)
        # need three distinct positive divisors sum to n
        # choose combinations of 3 distinct divisors
        for a in range(len(divs)):
            for b in range(a+1, len(divs)):
                for c in range(b+1, len(divs)):
                    if divs[a] + divs[b] + divs[c] == n:
                        return k
    return None

def compute_f_up_to(N, limit_factor=5):
    res = {}
    for n in range(6, N+1):
        # set search limit maybe n*limit_factor
        limit = max(100, n*limit_factor)
        val = f_bruteforce(n, limit=limit)
        if val is None:
            # increase limit
            val = f_bruteforce(n, limit=limit*5)
        res[n]=val
    return res

def f_bruteforce_limit3(n, maxk=5000):
    return f_bruteforce(n, limit=maxk)

def max_ratio(limit_n, maxk):
    maxr=0
    maxpair=None
    for n in range(6, limit_n+1):
        k=f_bruteforce(n, limit=maxk)
        if k is None:
            continue
        r=k/n
        if r>maxr:
            maxr=r
            maxpair=(n,k)
    return maxr, maxpair

max_ratio(500, 5000)

def is_power_of_three(x):
    if x <= 0:
        return False
    while x % 3 == 0:
        x //= 3
    return x == 1

[CODE] (turn 13):
def is_sum_two_powers_of_three(c):
    # find distinct a,b >=0 such that 3**a + 3**b == c
    powers = []
    i=0
    while 3**i <= c:
        powers.append(3**i)
        i+=1
    s=set(powers)
    for a in powers:
        b = c - a
        if b in s and b!=a:
            return True, a, b
    return False, None, None

c_vals = [0, 1848374, 10162574, 265710644, 44636594]
for c in c_vals:
    ok, a, b = is_sum_two_powers_of_three(c)
    print(c, ok, a, b)

[CODE] (turn 15):
def find_three_divisors_sum(k, target):
    divs = sp.divisors(k)
    for i in range(len(divs)):
        for j in range(i+1, len(divs)):
            for l in range(j+1, len(divs)):
                if divs[i]+divs[j]+divs[l]==target:
                    return (divs[i],divs[j],divs[l])
    return None

print(find_three_divisors_sum(18,27))

def test_nine(limit):
    mismatches=[]
    for n in range(9, limit+1, 9):

[REASONING] (turn 1):
I think the tool may be disabled. Let's attempt to use reasoning without code.

We can compute small primes manually using modular exponent properties.

We need to compute (M-1) mod p for small p.

Recall M = 3^{K}, K = 2025!.

For prime p not dividing 3, we have 3^{φ(p)} ≡ 1 mod p, where φ(p) = p-1. So 3^{K} mod p = 3^{K mod (p-1)} mod p.

Thus we need K mod (p-1). Since K = 2025! is divisible by (p-1) for any p-1 ≤ 2025. For primes p ≤ 2026, p-1 ≤ 2025, so K ≡ 0 mod (p-1). Therefore 3^{K} ≡ 3^{0} ≡ 1 mod p. Thus for any prime p ≤ 2026 (and p≠3), M ≡ 1 mod p. For p=3, M ≡ 0 mod3.

Thus for any prime p ≤ 2026, M-1 ≡ 0 mod p (except p=3 where M-1 ≡ -1 mod3 ≡ 2). So M-1 is divisible by all primes ≤2026 except 3. Wait need to check: For p=2, p-1=1 divides K, so 3^{K} ≡ 1 mod2, but 3 mod2 =1, so M ≡1 mod2, but M is odd, so M-1 ≡0 mod2. Indeed M-1 even.

For p=5, p-1=4 divides K, so M ≡1 mod5, M-1 ≡0 mod5. So 5 divides M-1.

Similarly for p=7, p-1=6 divides K, so M ≡1 mod7, M-1 ≡0 mod7.

Thus M-1 is divisible by all primes p ≤ 2026, p ≠3. Also divisible by powers of those primes up to exponent determined by K's multiplicity? Actually M-1 is not necessarily divisible by high powers; but at least divisible by each prime.

Thus the smallest divisor >2 of X = (M-1) + c depends on c mod p for small p.

Since (M-1) ≡ 0 mod p for many p, X mod p = c mod p.

Thus for c small, the smallest prime divisor >2 of X will be the smallest prime p>2 such that c ≡ 0 mod p, unless c mod p = 0 leads to X divisible by p.

But also need to consider p=2: X even? For c even, X even; but 2 is not allowed (must be >2). So we look for smallest odd prime dividing X.

Thus for each c, we need smallest odd prime p such that c ≡ 0 mod p (since (M-1) ≡0 mod p). However if c is not divisible by p, then X mod p = c mod p ≠0, so p does not divide X.

Thus the smallest odd prime divisor of X is the smallest odd prime divisor of c (since (M-1) is divisible by all primes up to 2026). But careful: If c has a prime factor >2026, then that prime may not divide M-1, so X mod p = (M-1) mod p + c mod p. Since (M-1) mod p may not be 0. However for primes up to 2026, (M-1) ≡0, so X mod p = c mod p. So any small prime dividing c will also divide X.

Thus for c values less than 2026? Some are larger. But we only need smallest divisor >2, which will be the smallest odd prime factor of c, unless c is even (then 2 divides X, but not allowed). If c is even, we need to consider if X divisible by 4? For c even, X = (M-1) + even. Since M-1 divisible by 4, X parity of mod4 depends on c mod4. If c mod4 =0, X divisible by 4, then smallest divisor >2 could be 4. If c mod4 =2, X not divisible by 4, but divisible by 2. Then need smallest odd divisor >2, which would be smallest odd prime factor of c/2? Actually X = (M-1) + c = (multiple of 4) + c. Since c ≡ 2 mod4, X ≡ 2 mod4, so X has exactly one factor of 2. So smallest divisor >2 will be smallest odd divisor of X. Since X ≡ (M-1) + c, and (M-1) is divisible by all odd primes ≤2026, X mod p = c mod p. So odd prime divisors of X correspond to odd prime divisors of c (if they are ≤2026). If c has odd prime factor >2026, then we need to compute (M-1) mod that prime.

Thus for each c, we can find smallest odd divisor >2 of X as follows:

- If c mod4 =0, then X divisible by 4, and smallest divisor >2 is 4 (since 4 < any odd prime >2). So s=4.

- If c mod4 =2, then X even but not divisible by 4. Then smallest divisor >2 is the smallest odd prime divisor of X. Since X = (M-1) + c, and (M-1) divisible by all odd primes ≤2026, X mod p = c mod p. So smallest odd prime divisor of X is smallest odd prime divisor of c (provided that prime ≤2026). If c has no odd prime divisor ≤2026 (i.e., c is a power of 2 times a large prime >2026), then we need to check that large prime.

Thus for c values with mod4=2, we need smallest odd prime divisor of c.

Let's factor each c:

c = 1848374. Divide by 2: 1848374 /2 = 924187. That's odd. So c = 2 * 924187. Need smallest odd prime factor of 924187.

Let's factor 924187. Check divisibility by 3: sum digits of 924187 = 9+2+4+1+8+7 = 31, not divisible by 3. By 5: no. By 7: 924187 /7 ≈ 132026.7. Compute 7*132026 = 924182, remainder 5, not divisible. By 11: 11*84017 = 924187? 11*84017 = 924187 exactly? Let's compute 84017*11 = 84017*10 + 84017 = 840170 + 84017 = 924187. Yes! So 924187 = 11 * 84017. So smallest odd prime factor is 11.

Thus for c = 1848374, smallest odd prime divisor of c is 11. So s = 11? Wait we need smallest divisor >2 of X. Since c mod4=2, X not divisible by 4. So s = smallest odd prime divisor of X, which equals smallest odd prime divisor of c, which is 11. So s = 11.

Thus d = X / s, and f = X * (s-1)/s.

Now compute X = M + c - 1 = (M - 1) + c.

We don't need explicit huge numbers; we can express f in terms of M and c.

Let X = (M - 1) + c = M + (c - 1). Then f = X * (s-1)/s = (M + c - 1) * (s-1)/s.

Thus f = (s-1)/s * (M + c - 1).

Now compute (2025! * f) / M = (2025! / M) * (s-1)/s * (M + c - 1) = 2025! * (s-1)/s * (1 + (c - 1)/M).

Since (c - 1)/M is tiny (<1), the term is negligible. So (2025! * f)/M = 2025! * (s-1)/s + epsilon, where epsilon = 2025! * (s-1)/s * (c - 1)/M.

Now we need floor of that. Since epsilon is positive but less than 1? Let's check magnitude: (c - 1)/M is extremely tiny (M huge). Multiply by 2025! which is about 10^{5818}, still negligible compared to M which is about 10^{~10^{5818}}? Actually M = 3^{K} with K ~ 10^{5818}, so log10 M = K * log10 3 ~ 0.477 * 10^{5818} huge. [CODE] (turn 2):
def min_ratio_for_s(s):
    min_ratio = None
    best = None
    for x in range(1, s-1):
        for y in range(x+1, s):
            z = s - x - y
            if z <= y: # ensure distinct and increasing
                continue
            # x<y<z distinct
            l = math.lcm(x, y, z)
            ratio = l / s
            if min_ratio is None or ratio < min_ratio:
                min_ratio = ratio
                best = (x,y,z,l)
    return min_ratio, best

for s in range(6, 31):
    r,b = min_ratio_for_s(s)
    print(s, r, b)

def global_min_ratio(limit):
    best = (None, None)
    for s in range(6, limit+1):
        r,_ = min_ratio_for_s(s)
        if best[0] is None or r < best[0]:
            best = (r, s)
    return best

def order_mod(a, m):
    for k in range(1, m):
        if pow(a, k, m) == 1:
            return k
    return None

def r_min(s):
    # compute minimal lcm(x,y,z)/s for distinct positive integers sum s
    min_ratio = None
    for x in range(1, s-1):
        for y in range(x+1, s):
            z = s - x - y
            if z <= y:
                continue
            l = math.lcm(x, y, z)
            ratio = l / s
            if min_ratio is None or ratio < min_ratio:
                min_ratio = ratio
    return min_ratio

# compute for s up to 200
ratios = {}
for s in range(6, 201):
    ratios[s] = r_min(s)

# find minimal ratio per s and also overall min
overall_min = min(ratios.values())
overall_min, [s for s,r in ratios.items() if r==overall_min][:10]

def r_min_for_power_of_three(limit_exp):
    results = {}
    for k in range(2, limit_exp+1):
        s = 3**k
        results[s] = r_min(s)
    return results

def find_min_ratio(limit_sum):
    best = (float('inf'), None)
    for a in range(1, limit_sum):
        for b in range(a+1, limit_sum):
            c = limit_sum - a - b
            if c <= b:
                continue
            l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            r = l / (a+b+c)
            if r < best[0]:
                best = (r, (a,b,c))
    return best

best = find_min_ratio(200)
best

def find_min_ratio_upto(max_sum):
    best = (float('inf'), None)
    for a in range(1, max_sum):
        for b in range(a+1, max_sum):

def find_second_min_ratio(max_sum):
    ratios = {}
    for a in range(1, max_sum):
        for b in range(a+1, max_sum):
            for c in range(b+1, max_sum):
                s = a+b+c
                if s > max_sum:
                    break
                l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
                r = l / s
                ratios.setdefault(r, []).append((a,b,c))
    sorted_ratios = sorted(ratios.items())
    # first ratio is min
    return sorted_ratios[:5]

find_second_min_ratio(100)

import math, sympy as sp

# define M mod for various moduli
mods = [7,10,11,9,13,14,16,22,33]  # etc
exp = sp.factorial(2025)  # huge, cannot compute directly. But we only need exponent modulo phi(mod)
# Instead compute exponent modulo phi for each modulus using properties: factorial contains all numbers up to 2025, so it's divisible by any integer <=2025.
def mod_pow_3(mod):
    if math.gcd(3, mod) == 1:
        phi = sp.totient(mod)
        # exponent is multiple of phi because factorial >= phi
        # Since factorial includes phi as factor, exponent mod phi = 0
        return pow(3, 0, mod)  # 1
    else:
        # mod divisible by 3
        # compute directly: if mod is power of 3 maybe
        # For mod=9, exponent >=2 => 0
        if mod == 9:
            return 0
        else:
            # handle other cases later
            return pow(3, 2025*2024//2, mod)  # placeholder
for m in mods:
    print(m, mod_pow_3(m))

import math, sympy as sp

# define M mod for various moduli
mods = [7,10,11,9,13,14,16,22,33]  # etc
exp = sp.factorial(2025)  # huge, cannot compute directly. But we only need exponent modulo phi(mod)
# Instead compute exponent modulo phi for each modulus using properties: factorial contains all numbers up to 2025, so it's divisible by any integer <=2025.
def mod_pow_3(mod):
    if math.gcd(3, mod) == 1:
        phi = sp.totient(mod)
        # exponent is multiple of phi because factorial >= phi
        # Since factorial includes phi as factor, exponent mod phi = 0
        return pow(3, 0, mod)  # 1
    else:
        # mod divisible by 3
        # compute directly: if mod is power of 3 maybe
        # For mod=9, exponent >=2 => 0
        if mod == 9:
            return 0
        else:
            # handle other cases later
            return pow(3, 2025*2024//2, mod)  # placeholder
for m in mods:

c_small = [0, 1848374, 10162574, 265710644, 44636594]
def N_mod(mod, idx):
    if idx == 0:
        # c=0
        return (1) % mod
    elif idx == 1:
        # N = 5M
        return (5 * 1) % mod
    else:
        # N = M + c_small[idx-1]
        return (1 + c_small[idx-1] % mod) % mod

def compute_divisible_by(N_mod_val, divisor):
    return N_mod_val % divisor == 0

mods_check = [11,7,10,9,13,14,16,22,33]
for idx in range(6):

def N_mod(mod, idx):
    if idx == 0:
        # c=0
        return (1) % mod
    elif idx == 1:
        # N = 5M
        return (5 * 1) % mod
    else:
        # N = M + c_small[idx-1]
        return (1 + c_small[idx-1] % mod) % mod

def compute_divisible_by(N_mod_val, divisor):

[CODE] (turn 17):
def find_ratios_upto_sum(max_sum):
    ratios = {}
    for a in range(1, max_sum):
        for b in range(a+1, max_sum):
            for c in range(b+1, max_sum):
                s = a+b+c
                if s > max_sum:
                    break
                l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
                r = sp.Rational(l, s)  # rational
                ratios.setdefault(r, []).append((a,b,c))
    return ratios

ratios = find_ratios_upto_sum(100)
# sort ratios
sorted_ratios = sorted(ratios.keys(), key=lambda x: float(x))
sorted_ratios[:20]

[CODE] (turn 20):
def min_ratio_for_sum(s):
    min_ratio = None
    best = None
    for a in range(1, s):
        for b in range(a+1, s):
            c = s - a - b
            if c <= b:
                continue
            l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            r = sp.Rational(l, s)
            if min_ratio is None or r < min_ratio:
                min_ratio = r
                best = (a,b,c,l)
    return min_ratio, best

def r_min_bruteforce(s):
    # compute minimal ratio for s using brute force but maybe heavy for s=45
    min_ratio = float('inf')
    best = None
    for a in range(1, s-1):
        for b in range(a+1, s):
            c = s - a - b
            if c <= b:
                continue
            l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            r = l / s
            if r < min_ratio:
                min_ratio = r
                best = (a,b,c,l)
    return min_ratio, best

    best = None

            c = s - a - b

    min_ratios = {}
    for s in range(6, limit+1):

        min_ratios[s] = (r,b)
    return min_ratios

import math
from fractions import Fraction

def min_ratio_fraction(s):
    min_frac = None
    best = None
    for a in range(1, s-1):
        for b in range(a+1, s):
            c = s - a - b
            if c <= b:
                continue
            l = math.lcm(a,b,c)
            frac = Fraction(l, s)
            if min_frac is None or frac < min_frac:
                min_frac = frac
                best = (a,b,c,l)
    return min_frac, best

def r_of_N(idx, max_s=500):
    # idx: 0 for M, 1 for 5M, 2 for M+1848374, etc.
    best_frac = None
    best_s = None
    best_trip = None
    for s in range(6, max_s+1):
        if N_mod_general(s, idx) == 0:
            frac, trip = min_ratio_fraction(s)
            if best_frac is None or frac < best_frac:
                best_frac = frac
                best_s = s
                best_trip = trip
    return best_frac, best_s, best_trip

# compute for each idx
results = {}
for idx in range(6):
    frac, s, trip = r_of_N(idx, max_s=500)
    results[idx] = (frac, s, trip)
    print(idx, frac, s, trip)

    # find smallest odd prime p such that (3^K + c - 1) % p == 0
    # c may be integer (could be huge like 4M)
    # For c huge, we need to compute expression modulo p using pow.
    # If c is multiple of M (3^K), we can reduce c modulo p as well.
    # We'll treat c as integer (maybe huge) but we can compute c mod p using pow if needed.
    # For c = 4M, we will pass c as integer 4*pow(3,K) maybe huge; but we can compute (c_mod_p) = (4 * pow(3,K,p)) % p.
    # So we need to handle c as expression: either integer small or multiple of M.
    # We'll pass a function that given p returns value of (3^K + c - 1) mod p.
    pass

[CODE] (turn 4):
def smallest_odd_prime_divisor(c, multM=False):
    # c: integer (if multM=False) or integer multiplier (if multM=True) meaning c = multiplier * M
    # returns smallest odd prime divisor p of N = M + c - 1 (if multM=False)
    # or N = M + multiplier*M - 1 = (1+multiplier)*M - 1 if multM=True
    # Actually for multM case, c is multiplier (e.g., 4) meaning c = multiplier * M.
    # So N = M + multiplier*M - 1 = (1+multiplier)*M - 1.
    # We'll compute modulo p using pow(3,K,p).
    # Search over odd primes starting from 3.
    for p in sp.primerange(3, 1000000):  # search up to 1e6 maybe enough
        if multM:
            # N = (1+multiplier)*M - 1
            mult = c + 1  # multiplier+1
            val = (mult * pow(3, K, p) - 1) % p
        else:
            val = (pow(3, K, p) + c - 1) % p
        if val == 0:
            return p
    return None

# test for c=0
d0 = smallest_odd_prime_divisor(0, multM=False)
d0

[CODE] (turn 6):
# Precompute pow3_mod for primes up to some limit
def compute_smallest_primes_for_cs(cs, multM_multiplier=None, limit=200000):
    # cs: list of c integers (small)
    # multM_multiplier: if not None, handle case where c = multiplier * M (like 4)
    # returns dict c->smallest odd prime divisor
    results = {}
    # generate odd primes up to limit
    primes = list(sp.primerange(3, limit))
    # precompute pow3_mod for each prime
    pow3_mod = {p: pow(3, K, p) for p in primes}
    # for each c, find smallest p where condition holds
    for c in cs:
        for p in primes:
            val = (pow3_mod[p] + c - 1) % p
            if val == 0:
                results[c] = p
                break
        else:
            results[c] = None
    if multM_multiplier is not None:
        # handle N = (1+multiplier)*M - 1
        mult = multM_multiplier + 1
        for p in primes:
            val = (mult * pow3_mod[p] - 1) % p
            if val == 0:
                results['mult'] = p
                break
        else:
            results['mult'] = None
    return results

cs = [0, 1848374, 10162574, 265710644, 44636594]
res = compute_smallest_primes_for_cs(cs, multM_multiplier=4, limit=200000)
res

# Precompute pow3_mod for primes up to some limit
def compute_smallest_primes_for_cs(cs, multM_multiplier=None, limit=200000):
    # cs: list of c integers (small)
    # multM_multiplier: if not None, handle case where c = multiplier * M (like 4)
    # returns dict c->smallest odd prime divisor
    results = {}
    # generate odd primes up to limit
    primes = list(sp.primerange(3, limit))
    # precompute pow3_mod for each prime
    pow3_mod = {p: pow(3, K, p) for p in primes}
    # for each c, find smallest p where condition holds
    for c in cs:
        for p in primes:
            val = (pow3_mod[p] + c - 1) % p
            if val == 0:
                results[c] = p
                break
        else:
            results[c] = None
    if multM_multiplier is not None:
        # handle N = (1+multiplier)*M - 1
        mult = multM_multiplier + 1
        for p in primes:
            val = (mult * pow3_mod[p] - 1) % p
            if val == 0:
                results['mult'] = p
                break
        else:
            results['mult'] = None
    return results

[CODE] (turn 7):
import sympy as sp, math
K = math.factorial(2025)
def compute_smallest_primes_for_cs(cs, multM_multiplier=None, limit=200000):
    results = {}
    primes = list(sp.primerange(3, limit))
    pow3_mod = {p: pow(3, K, p) for p in primes}
    for c in cs:
        for p in primes:
            if (pow3_mod[p] + c - 1) % p == 0:
                results[c] = p
                break
        else:
            results[c] = None
    if multM_multiplier is not None:
        mult = multM_multiplier + 1
        for p in primes:
            if (mult * pow3_mod[p] - 1) % p == 0:
                results['mult'] = p
                break
        else:
            results['mult'] = None
    return results

cs = [0, 1848374, 10162574, 265710644, 44636594]
res = compute_smallest_primes_for_cs(cs, multM_multiplier=4, limit=200000)
res
